#3072. Intuit (INTU) Q1 2027 Earnings Call Transcript

Mailchimp sale proceeds fund buybacks as AI reshapes consumer financial discovery.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 20, 2026 at 10:14AM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Intuit (INTU -3.83%) reported first-quarter results exceeding its previous guidance and announced a definitive agreement to sell its Mailchimp segment for $3.68 billion to Bending Spoons. Management reported that the divestiture will sharpen focus on AI-native lead-to-cash capabilities within the QuickBooks platform. While the Global Business Solutions segment saw revenue growth of 15%, the company lowered its full-year outlook for Credit Karma due to macroeconomic headwinds and a shift in how consumers discover financial products through AI platforms. The company intends to deploy the cash proceeds from the Mailchimp sale toward additional share repurchases during fiscal 2027.

Operator: Good afternoon. My name is Ronnie, and I will be your conference operator. At this time, I would like to welcome everyone to Intuit's First Quarter Fiscal Year 2027 Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] With that, I'll now turn the call over to Kendra Goodenough, Intuit's Vice President of Investor Relations. Ms. Goodenough?

Kendra Goodenough: Good afternoon, and welcome to Intuit's First Quarter Fiscal 2027 Conference Call. I'm here with Intuit's CEO, Sasan Goodarzi; and our CFO, Sandeep Aujla. Before we start, I'd like to remind everyone that our remarks will include forward-looking statements. There are a number of factors that could cause Intuit's results to differ materially from our expectations. You can learn more about these risks in the press release we issued earlier this afternoon, our Form 10-K for fiscal 2026 and our other SEC filings. All of these documents are available on the Investor Relations page of Intuit's website at intuit.com. We assume no obligation to update any forward-looking statement.

Some of the numbers in these remarks are presented on a non-GAAP basis. We've reconciled the comparable GAAP and non-GAAP numbers in today's press release. Unless otherwise noted, all growth rates refer to the current period versus the comparable prior year period, and the business metrics and associated growth rates refer to worldwide business metrics. A copy of our prepared remarks and supplemental financial information will be available on our website after this call ends. With that, I'll turn the call over to Sasan.

Sasan Goodarzi: Thanks, Kendra, and thanks to all of you for joining us today. We delivered Q1 revenue growth of 12%, with revenue, operating income and earnings per share above the top end of the guidance. Today, I will focus on 3 things. First, how we're positioning Intuit to win as an AI-driven expert platform, regardless of where customers choose to work. Second, the innovation we launched across our business platform this quarter to help businesses and accountants succeed, deepen our network effect and fuel growth across the platform. And third, what we learned from the tests we ran during the October tax extension season and the actions we're taking across Credit Karma and TurboTax to drive stronger customer growth.

I have a great deal of conviction in our strategy to win as an AI-driven expert platform. We bring together decades of trusted proprietary data across millions of businesses and consumers; deep financial and industry platform capabilities spanning 74 financial domains, more than 100 workflows and 307 industries; and an agentic orchestration layer of domain-specific models built for accuracy and compliance, working alongside human experts. Together, these capabilities increasingly do the work for our customers and help them achieve better outcomes. This is what we refer to as our system of intelligence, designed to fuel growth in the era of AI.

As we highlighted at Investor Day in September, our strategy is to meet customers wherever they choose to work, whether directly within our platform or through partners like OpenAI, Anthropic, Microsoft and Google. When it comes to financial intelligence and action, Intuit is the system of intelligence that powers the outcome. These AI tools and agents are becoming new front doors for discovery, much like search engines, mobile devices and app marketplaces before them. They help customers ask questions, discover solutions and get started. When the need becomes financial decisions and actions, Intuit brings the trusted data, deep domain expertise and financial system of intelligence to turn that intent into intelligence and action, accurately, securely and at scale.

That's why partners seek out Intuit and why we've built experiences with every leading AI platform. We recently signed 3 more partnerships with Meta, Microsoft and SpaceXSI. QuickBooks was an early partner for the launch of Meta's Muse for Small Business and Microsoft Copilot for Finance. That means businesses can connect their QuickBooks account inside these tools to manage invoices, get paid faster and better understand their business. QuickBooks is also available through Claude, ChatGPT and Perplexity, while TurboTax and Credit Karma are available inside Claude and ChatGPT, enabled by MCP connectors that extend the Intuit platform's trusted intelligence, data and actions into these experiences. These integrations are still early, but the results are encouraging.

Across the major AI tools and agents, our QuickBooks and TurboTax experiences are among the top ranked for discovery on key topics. TurboTax is the most prominent tax brand and ranked first most often when consumers ask about tax solutions to use, about 4x higher than the next tax brand. QuickBooks is also the most prominent finance brand for businesses and is listed most often as the first product choice. We have found that users already connecting their Intuit accounts through AI tool or agent tend to be the customers that are more engaged with our products. With QuickBooks customers, over 30% of those who connected their account to an AI tool or agent are engaging each week.

We're seeing over 2 million calls to QuickBooks per week and growing. Early indicators suggest that this engagement is translating into deeper adoption. QuickBooks customers who connect through AI tools and agents are showing higher rates of payroll, payments and bill pay adoption and plan upgrades than similar customers who are not connected. For example, sending invoices is one of the most used features in the QuickBooks Connector, and since launching in June, we've rapidly scaled to an annualized TPV run rate of over $1 billion as of this month.

Our objective is to create the future with AI-native experiences that fuel the success of customers by building and testing new experiences with our partners, iterating fast and scaling across new front doors. To bring this back to where I started, the front door may change, but wherever customers choose to work, Intuit will be there, powering the trusted intelligence and financial action that help them get the work done. Let's shift to the second topic, our business platform. For businesses and accountants, we're helping them grow and run their business end-to-end from accounting and human capital management to payments, bill pay and financing.

Across our business platform, our growth strategy is clear: bring more new customers onto the platform, from early entrepreneurs to mid-market businesses. And as their needs become more complex, we can grow with them over time as they adopt more money and workforce services. And across the platform, we're bringing that strategy to life with new experiences for businesses and accountants at every stage of growth. Last month, at Intuit Connect, our flagship event for leading accounting firms and mid-market businesses, we showcased our pipeline of innovation to fuel the success of businesses and accountants and strengthen the network effect across our platform. I'll highlight 3 areas: Intuit Intelligence, Intuit Accountant Suite and Intuit Enterprise Suite.

First, we showcased Intuit Intelligence, which brings businesses and accountants together on one unified platform, combining trusted, permissioned financial data, deep financial and industry expertise and the right intelligence for each task. The result is a platform that understands performance, automates work and takes action from running payroll and paying bills to sending invoices and extending capital. It increasingly turns insights and recommendations into completed work, helping businesses operate with greater speed and confidence. Second, for accountants, we highlighted new innovations for Intuit Accountant Suite, which is our AI-native, agnostic platform to help accountants manage their firm, their team and their clients. Intuit Accountant Suite now has more than 430,000 accountants on the platform.

At Intuit Connect, we announced Agentic Books Close, which automates month-end close using a firm's own standard operating procedures while accountants supervise and manage exceptions. We also introduced Firm Playbooks and Portfolio Intelligence to help firms standardize how they work and surface insights across their entire book of business. These capabilities build on the automation already available across our platform from categorizing transactions and surfacing anomalies to managing unpaid invoices, benchmarking against peers and turning recommendations into completed work. We also introduced ProPartner, which will launch in early 2027. We highlighted multiple innovations and benefits, one of them client matching to fuel accounting firms' growth. Client matching connects businesses to accountants on our platform.

Based on the needs of the business, our platform algorithms make a match, which shows up as a lead in Intuit Accountant Suite. This is great for businesses, as human intelligence is key to their success and fuels growth for firms on our platform, strengthening our network effect. As more businesses and accountants connect on the platform, we create more value and growth for both sides. Together, these innovations take work off a firm's plate, increase efficiency and create more capacity for higher-value advisory services and growth. For mid-market businesses, we now have more than 20,000 businesses on our platform that have annual revenue above $20 million.

At Intuit Connect, we showed how our industry-specific strategy is helping us deepen our penetration in this segment and remains a critical lever to upgrade existing customers and acquire new ones. We announced new editions of Intuit Enterprise Suite for manufacturing and nonprofit organizations, along with additional innovation for construction, all of which will be generally available soon. Each is tailored to the specific needs of its industry. Contractors get capabilities, including job costing, project budgets, proposals and progress-based invoicing. Manufacturers get workflows for multilocation inventory and production, and nonprofits get fund and grant accounting built directly into the platform. We're seeing strong engagement from our construction customers.

More than 70% use Project Financials every month, and 96% of those who engaged in September returned in October, demonstrating strong and sustained usage. What energized me most at Intuit Connect was the enthusiasm we heard directly from accountants and business leaders. They see the opportunity ahead. They're embracing the innovation we're bringing to market, and they are building the future alongside us. Now let's turn to results. GBS revenue grew 15% this quarter, driven by 20% growth in Online Ecosystem. Let me highlight what we're seeing across mid-market, early-stage businesses and money. In mid-market, Online Ecosystem revenue for QBO Advanced and Intuit Enterprise Suite accelerated to 41% with Intuit Enterprise Suite reaching $177 million in annualized revenue.

Mid-market customers grew 30% in Q1, driven by QBO Advanced, which benefited from the success of our August promotional pricing. We also saw strong momentum in new customer acquisition, with Intuit Enterprise Suite contract growth of 145% in the quarter. And accountants continue to be an important part of our mid-market growth. They drove 20% of new Intuit Enterprise Suite contracts in Q1. And as we scale, the network effect between businesses and accountants only strengthens. We're also expanding our platform with the acquisition of Sequence, an AI-native revenue management platform that automates the entire quote-to-revenue process. Sequence will deepen our capabilities in industries like construction and help us serve more mid-market businesses with complex revenue models.

Now shifting to early-stage businesses. QuickBooks Free and QuickBooks Lite give us low-friction entry points to reach millions of businesses earlier in their journey. Among customers who signed up in July and August and subsequently upgraded after seeing value in the platform, approximately 80% did so within the first 30 days, with more than 2/3 upgrading within the first 2 weeks. Our money offerings deepen the relationship with customers and allow us to monetize in multiple ways based on the benefits customers care about most. In their first month, customers who connect a bank or send an invoice are 9x and 4x more likely, respectively, to upgrade to a paid account compared to customers who don't.

As we expand the range of businesses we serve from early-stage to mid-market, putting money at the center of the platform allows us to grow with customers as their needs become more complex. Increasing adoption of our money offerings drove 33% growth in money -- in business money total payment volume, which includes payments, bill pay and buy now, pay later. Turning to our consumer platform. Our financial system of intelligence helps consumers make smarter financial decisions year-round, whether connecting them to the right credit cards, personal loans, auto loans, home loans and insurance, helping them manage their money or filing their taxes.

Our growth opportunity is to use Credit Karma and TurboTax as a powerful entry point to bring more consumers onto the platform, grow our share of total IRS e-filers and deepen those relationships across their financial lives, driving higher engagement and platform ARPC over time. Let me begin with Credit Karma. Revenue grew 9% with higher monetization from personal loans and insurance. However, performance was below our expectations, reflecting the impact of higher interest rates on our growth and a faster-than-anticipated impact from a shift in consumer behavior. We see consumers increasingly starting in AI-powered experiences to search for answers and discover financial products.

We're taking what we have learned from TurboTax and QuickBooks, which are top choices and applying those best practices to accelerate our efforts to also make Credit Karma a top choice in AI-powered experiences. As we shared at Investor Day, our strategy is to evolve how consumers think about and use Credit Karma from a place they go to monitor their credit to a platform they rely on more frequently to manage their financial lives. This quarter, we launched a brand campaign that positions Credit Karma as an AI-powered daily financial platform to help members save, spend and grow their money throughout their financial journey. Turning to tax.

As we have previously highlighted, we are evolving our tax growth model to regain share of total IRS e-filers and scale assisted tax by growing new-to-the-franchise filers. Let me start with Credit Karma Tax, which we announced this quarter. It is built natively inside Credit Karma so members get a one-click start and their data comes over automatically. This coming season, we are offering free federal filing and flat rate $15 state filing to all members with real-time AI help, 100% accuracy guarantee and federal refunds up to 5 days early with a Credit Karma checking account. This aligns with our strategy to use tax as a powerful new-to-the-franchise entry point.

During the extension season pilot, 70% of the Credit Karma Tax customers were new to Intuit, giving us an opportunity to bring more consumers onto the platform and deepen the relationship over time. For our assisted offerings, which target approximately 88% of our total tax addressable market, we tested Sign and File, an AI-native virtual experience for customers who want to prepare most of their return themselves, with the added confidence and accountability of an expert. The customer prepares their return with AI, then a TurboTax expert reviews, signs and files it. In early testing with customers using our Expert Assist product, roughly twice as many opted into Sign and File versus expert final review alone.

That matters because every year about 14 million assisted filers switch to a new preparer, with roughly 1/3 driven by price. This is why we're offering Sign and File at a disruptive price point. The extension season gave us encouraging signals, and we are focused on applying those learnings as we continue to strengthen the consumer platform and position it for durable long-term growth. Before I wrap up, I want to address a decision we announced earlier today. We have signed an agreement to sell Mailchimp to Bending Spoons.

Mailchimp has helped millions of businesses grow and acquire customers through marketing automation, and I'm incredibly grateful to the Mailchimp team for what they have built and the impact that they've had on our customers and on Intuit. Our commitment to our customers has not changed. We remain focused on solving their biggest problems and helping them run and grow their business on our end-to-end platform. We have built AI-native experiences to help businesses win new customers, manage their pipeline and help them grow through our lead-to-cash capabilities. Selling Mailchimp sharpens our focus behind that strategy, while positioning Mailchimp and its team for continued success in their next chapter with Bending Spoons.

To close, fiscal year 2027 is about executing our plan with greater speed and discipline and strengthening our competitive position. We are deliberately focused on growing new customers to capture market share and increasing adoption of platform services to drive higher ARPC, while we continue to scale our Big Bets. We know where we need to deliver and our focus is on executing with consistency while continuing to bring powerful innovation to the market. With that, let me turn it over to Sandeep.

Sandeep Aujla: Thanks, Sasan. Our results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027 include: revenue of $4.4 billion, up 12%; GAAP operating income of $0.9 billion, up 63%; non-GAAP operating income of $1 billion, up 46%; GAAP diluted earnings per share of $2.59, up 63%; and non-GAAP diluted earnings per share of $2.91, up 56%. Now let me turn to the business segment results, starting with the Global Business Solutions segment. GBS revenue grew 15% during the quarter, driven by Online Ecosystem, which grew 20%. We saw continued momentum in mid-market again this quarter, with Online Ecosystem revenue for QBO Advanced and Intuit Enterprise Suite growing 41%.

Online Ecosystem revenue for small businesses and the rest of the base grew 15% this quarter. Our overall growth in Online Ecosystem revenue was driven by strength across both online accounting and online services. QuickBooks Online accounting revenue grew 19%, driven by higher effective prices, mix shift and customer growth. Online services revenue grew 21%, driven by money and payroll. Within money, revenue growth in the quarter was driven by payments, including bill pay as well as capital. Payments revenue growth reflects an increase in total payment volume per customer and customer growth. Business money total payment volume, which includes payments, bill pay and buy now, pay later, grew 33% in the quarter, reflecting continued adoption of our money offerings.

In capital, revenue growth continues to be driven by volume of working capital loans to small businesses. Within payroll, revenue growth in the quarter reflects mix shift, higher effective prices and customer growth as we continue to see strong adoption of our HCM offerings. Turning to desktop. Desktop ecosystem revenue grew 2%, with the Q1 deceleration driven by continued customer migration to our online offerings. Next, Mailchimp. Revenue was down slightly versus a year ago, in line with our expectations. Now turning to our Consumer segment, where revenue grew 7%. Credit Karma revenue grew 9%, driven by personal loans and insurance, partially offset by credit cards. Credit Karma performance was below our internal expectations for both revenue and engagement.

As Sasan noted, we saw impact from external factors including higher-than-expected interest rates that impacted our growth later in the quarter, and more broadly we saw a faster-than-anticipated impact from a shift in consumer behavior toward AI-powered experiences. The unfavorable revenue impact was partially offset by a favorable rate adjustment from a partner that will benefit growth in personal loans this year. TurboTax revenue grew 1%, roughly in line with expectations as the continued growth and expansion of assisted tax was mostly offset by the impact from year-over-year differences in disaster-related filing extensions in impacted counties. ProTax revenue grew 13%.

As Sasan mentioned, the results from our go-to-market and product experience tests we ran during the tax extension season in Q1 will inform our strategy for this upcoming tax season. Our goals are clear: use TurboTax and Credit Karma as powerful entry points to bring more consumers into the platform, grow our share of total IRS e-filers and engage more broadly in consumers' financial lives, driving higher platform ARPC over time. Shifting to our balance sheet and capital allocation. Our financial principles guide our decisions and they remain our long-term commitment. We finished the quarter with approximately $5.3 billion in cash and investments and $6.9 billion in total debt on our balance sheet.

We repurchased $1.5 billion of stock during the first quarter, an increase of 73% compared to the same period last year. Share repurchases remain a significant component of our capital allocation strategy in fiscal 2027 and beyond. And in addition to our planned repurchase strategy, we expect to use cash proceeds from the sale of Mailchimp to repurchase additional shares within fiscal 2027 following the close of the transaction. The Board approved a quarterly dividend of $1.38 per share payable on January 15, 2027. This represents a 15% increase versus last year. Now moving on to guidance.

All guidance for both next quarter and the fiscal year does not include any impact from pending sale of Mailchimp, which we expect to close in early calendar year 2027. We will update guidance at the time of close. First, Q2 guidance. We continue to make progress executing against our strategy to scale our Big Bets and accelerate new customer growth. At the same time, there are factors that will influence Q2 revenue growth outlook that I'd like to unpack. We expect total company revenue growth of 6% to 7%.

Starting with Global Business Solutions, we expect revenue growth to be in the low to mid-teens, with continued momentum in our Online Ecosystem, which we expect to grow in the mid- to high teens. In desktop, we expect revenue to be roughly flat year-over-year, decelerating from last quarter which is consistent with our full year expectations for a low single-digit decline. Shifting to the Consumer segment. We expect revenue to decline in the mid-single digits, reflecting the deliberate actions we are taking to grow share in tax and the recent trends we are seeing in Credit Karma.

Starting with TurboTax, as Sasan shared, our priority is to grow our share of total IRS e-filers by improving our price-to-value proposition, particularly for the price-sensitive consumer while continuing to scale in the assisted tax category that represents approximately 88% of our tax addressable market. As previously shared, we expect these actions to lower tax ARPC in fiscal 2027, with greater impact in Q2 given the higher mix of price-sensitive customers choosing free offerings earlier in the tax season. We expect these decisions will strengthen new customer acquisition and create opportunities for deeper engagement and monetization across the consumer platform over time.

Additionally, we have strategically decided to move our refund transfer processing in-house to improve the customer experience and increase unit economics. This shifts revenue recognition from IRS acceptance of the return to IRS issuance of the refund, and we expect this change will shift approximately $30 million from Q2 to Q3. As a result of these factors, we expect TurboTax Q2 revenue to be down year-over-year. Turning to Credit Karma. As I mentioned earlier, in Q1, we saw higher interest rates weigh on growth as well as impact from a shift in consumer behavior. Our Q2 guidance assumes the demand environment remains at current levels.

We are focused on improving engagement and execution across the platform, while incorporating the recent demand environment into our outlook. And in Mailchimp, the revenue decline year-over-year should be similar to last quarter. We expect GAAP earnings per share of $2.55 to $2.60, and non-GAAP earnings per share of $3.17 to $3.22. Next, turning to our guidance for the full fiscal year 2027. Based on the Credit Karma performance and trends we have discussed, we are updating our full year revenue outlook. This update is limited to Credit Karma, with corresponding adjustments to Consumer segment and total company revenue guidance.

Therefore, full year guidance now includes total company revenue of $23.081 billion to $23.365 billion or growth of 8% to 9%. We continue to expect Global Business Solutions segment revenue growth of 13% to 14%. We now expect Consumer segment revenue growth of 2% to 4%. Within the Consumer segment, we expect TurboTax growth of 2% to 3%, Credit Karma growth of 3% to 7% and ProTax growth of approximately 2%. And we expect Mailchimp revenue growth to be flat to down 1% year-over-year. For operating income, we are reiterating our full year guidance as cost savings primarily from lower-than-expected share-based compensation expense will offset the impact from lower Credit Karma revenue.

Therefore, we continue to expect GAAP operating income growth of 26% to 27% and non-GAAP operating income growth of 17% to 18%. We are also reiterating our earnings per share guidance with GAAP diluted earnings per share expected to be $20.12 to $20.36 or growth of 22% to 24%, and non-GAAP diluted earnings per share expected to be $22.88 to $23.12 or growth of 23% to 24%. And lastly, we continue to expect a GAAP tax rate of approximately 27% in fiscal 2027. You can find our Q2 and full year 2027 guidance details in our press release and on our fact sheet. With that, I'll turn it back over to Sasan.

Sasan Goodarzi: Great. Thank you, Sandeep. We have spent years building Intuit to lead in this AI era, and the opportunity in front of us is significant: an addressable market of more than $300 billion where we have roughly 7% penetration, customers with increasingly complex demands and needs, and technology that allows us to solve these needs in a fundamentally new way. Our focus now is on turning that opportunity into durable growth. We're clear on where we need to be investing, and now it comes down to execution. With that, let's open it up to your questions.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] Our first question will come from Adam Wood with Morgan Stanley.

Adam Wood: I wanted to ask about the Credit Karma change, maybe just in kind of 2 ways. First of all, could you talk about how you catch up in the rest of the kind of 3-year guidance period on the rest of the consumer business and the reset this year in Credit Karma? And maybe more generally on the strategy around being able to monetize tax effectively through Credit Karma. How does that change strategically when it's a little bit more challenging, at least this year, for you to monetize in Credit Karma itself?

Sasan Goodarzi: Great. Maybe if I could, I'll start with your question around the consumer strategy, particularly tax, and I'll turn it over to Sandeep on your question around guidance. First and foremost, nothing changes, given what we just articulated. But let me just, as a reminder, share that our focus is really to help customers from building credit to ultimately building wealth. And we have deeply integrated at the right touchpoints the capabilities across Credit Karma and TurboTax. And the positioning that we have of Credit Karma in terms of going after tax customers is really customers that are simple filers.

And I think the stats that I shared earlier, which was a learning from our October tax season extension, really is an important proof that this is playing out as we would want. Now we need to demonstrate it in tax season, and that is 70% of the customers that came in through CK Tax were new to the franchise. And that's important on 2 dimensions. One, it brings new customers to the platform that we can then monetize beyond tax, which I think is the essence of your question. And two, it positions us for tax share growth. Now we need to prove that out during tax season, but this is very, very encouraging.

In terms of your specific question around monetization, I would take you back to sort of the 3 big goals that we have. We want to increase total IRS share of e-filings. We want to continue to scale our assisted tax business, which is a multibillion-dollar business growing quite rapidly. And third, we want to grow our ARPC revenue across the platform. And so as we bring these tax customers in, there are multiple ways that we can deliver benefits and monetize. It's first and foremost financial products that they may need that are personalized and specific to them, like cards, loans, insurance.

It's also money benefits, like early access to money, the fact that they can deposit their earnings in a checking account that we can then monetize. So it's a spectrum of ways we can monetize. I think the most important thing I would just end with is the acquisition of new customers. And we believe that based on the learnings that we've had and the strategy that we have, that CK Tax is an important trigger to accelerate new customer growth. Let me ask Sandeep to touch on the guidance question that you asked.

Sandeep Aujla: Yes, Adam, we continue to be confident in the 3-year CAGR that we put out for the consumer business of 4% to 8%. We've always known, and we've shared in the past as well that we know CK is the one part of our business that does have exposure to the macro environment. And the guidance that I shared today, the updated guidance for the year and Q2, we're taking the current environment into consideration in that outlook. And we also know the work we need to do and are confident that our team will go address that work.

We know in AI placement, we are really strong when it comes to TurboTax and QuickBooks, and taking those same learnings, applying them to Credit Karma, will help us get that better placement over time. And as you think about the 4% to 8% 3-year CAGR for consumer, the biggest factor there continues to be assisted tax. That is where the majority of the consumer and the tax revenue lies, and that is the top thing you have to underwrite to get that 4% to 8% outcome.

Operator: Your next question will come from Brad Zelnick with Deutsche Bank.

Brad Zelnick: Awesome. Really appreciate the question. It follows Adam's. I just was hoping you could expand on the recent trends that you're seeing in Credit Karma. Sasan Goodarzi: Yes, Brad, let me start, and of course, Sandeep will add to this. First and foremost, there are 2 areas where we've seen impact that really started towards the end of the quarter. One is the demand growth rate slowing because of the interest rate changes. So that's one. And this is an area where we have a lot of best practices. It's why when you look at TurboTax and QuickBooks, they're actually one of the top choices when you search within the LLMs. And that actually not only drives good intent traffic to us, but also it's the experiences that we're building within the LLMs where customers can use our capabilities to achieve prosperity in whatever is important to them.

When we think about Credit Karma, this is something, by the way, we've been working on with Credit Karma for quite some time, just the faster impact that we anticipated to what we saw in Credit Karma. And that is Credit Karma typically, very little of the traffic was dependent on SEO. And when more and more customers are doing their discovery work within the LLMs, we need to also make sure we show up in those LLMs. So we've been doing work for some time within Credit Karma to make sure that we apply the best practices across QuickBooks and TurboTax to Credit Karma. And we -- our teams are all over this.

It's just the impact was faster than we anticipated, and we expect to be in a better place in the coming months because, again, we know how to do this. We're applying the same best practices to Credit Karma.

Sandeep Aujla: And Brad, to add to that, your questions around the Credit Karma cross-sell to CK Tax, as we've shared in the past, we have a lot of confidence that our holistic consumer platform, as we get these customers in through CK Tax, they will see benefits and will drive strong LTV. Not only they've done it, but they're accountable for it.

And so the reason Sign and File for us is important from a lot of learning and testing that we did last year and in our October tax extension season is that there are 14 million folks within the assisted segment that switch every year, and some really switch entirely based on price. And there's a segment of these customers that actually want to do some of the taxes themselves, but then at the end want to hand it over to someone to finish it. And that's what Sign and File is. And that's the work our team has been doing, and I expect that to start paying out the benefits that we need as we go ahead. The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#3068. 3 Reasons to Claim Social Security at 67

There are fewer restrictions if you wait until 67 for most retirees.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 20, 2026 at 10:15AM EST

Few financial decisions near retirement are as important as deciding when to claim Social Security. With many retirees relying on it for most (if not all) of their retirement income, the timing of your claim can affect your financial standing.

You can begin claiming benefits as early as age 62, but claiming before your full retirement age permanently reduces your benefits. For anyone born in 1960 or later, the full retirement age is 67.

With the option to claim early or delay benefits until age 70, should you claim at 67? Here are three reasons to do so.

Your full retirement age is when you're eligible to receive your full monthly benefit, called your primary insurance amount (PIA). If you claim benefits before your full retirement age, Social Security will reduce your monthly benefit based on how far away you are from your full retirement age.

If you're within 36 months, your benefit is reduced by five-ninths of 1% for each month early. Each month after 36 months, your benefits decrease by five-twelfths of 1%.

Assuming your full retirement age is 67, claiming at 62 would result in a benefit that's 30% lower than your PIA. Claiming at 64 would mean one that's 20% less.

If you delay benefits past your full retirement age, they increase by two-thirds of 1% per month (8% annually) until you turn 70.

Social Security spousal benefits allow someone to claim based on their spouse's work history rather than their own. In addition to being 62 years old and having been married for at least a year, one requirement for claiming spousal benefits is that the primary spouse must currently be receiving benefits.

If your spouse plans to claim spousal benefits, you claiming at 67 means they can start receiving theirs right away, rather than potentially having to wait if you decide to delay benefits. The person claiming spousal benefits can receive up to 50% of their spouse's PIA, so even if you delay benefits past 67, your partner wouldn't receive a larger benefit than if you claimed at 67.

If you're set on delaying benefits past 67, your spouse can claim their own benefits to begin and then apply for the higher spousal benefits later (if spousal benefits aren't higher, there's no need to go that route).

If you claim benefits before full retirement age and keep working and earning money, you'll need to watch how much you earn, or you could be subject to Social Security's retirement earnings test (RET).

In 2027, if you won't reach your full retirement age, the earnings limit is $25,560. Earning more than that reduces your benefit by $1 for every $2 over that amount. If you'll reach full retirement age in 2027, the earnings limit is $67,920. Going over that reduces benefits by $1 for every $3 over.

Benefits are gradually returned once you reach your full retirement age, but if you wait until full retirement age to claim benefits, you won't have to worry about the retirement earnings test at all. You're free to earn as much as you want without any reduction in benefits (which is more like withholding).

#3063. Why Strategy Stock Tanked 23% This Week

Strategy shares now trade below the value of the company’s Bitcoin. Is that a bargain or a warning sign?

By Anders Bylund – Nov 20, 2026 at 10:17AM EST

A drop in Bitcoin below $62,000, combined with $244.7 million in common stock sales, sent Strategy tumbling this week.

The stock dropped 22.8% from Friday, Nov. 13's close through Thursday, Nov. 19, trading at $86.13 as of the close on Thursday, Nov. 19. Over the same period, the S&P 500 (^GSPC +0.02%) fell 5.1%, and the Nasdaq-100 was down 5.1%.

Strategy disclosed in a Securities and Exchange Commission filing on Monday that it raised $244.7 million by selling common shares between Nov. 9 and Nov. 15. Management allocated $206.3 million of those proceeds to repurchase its preferred stock, while spending just $33.4 million to add 485 Bitcoin (BTC -3.67%). The dilution added pressure to a stock already struggling as Bitcoin fell to four-month lows, leaving Strategy’s shares down 57.8% (as of the close on Thursday, Nov. 19) from their 52-week high of $203.95.

Strategy sells business analytics software to corporate clients while operating primarily as a treasury company that acquires and holds Bitcoin on its balance sheet. In its last reported quarter, which ended Sept. 30, 2026, software revenue slipped 2.5% year over year to $125.4 million. The company held 850,849 Bitcoin on Nov. 19. Expanding the common share count dilutes existing investors' claim on those cryptocurrency holdings when proceeds fund preferred share repurchases rather than new coin purchases.

Analysts at Monness Crespi downgraded the stock from Neutral to Sell with a $65 price target, citing dilution risks. At the same time, index provider MSCI scheduled the stock's removal from its benchmarks after the Nov. 30 market close. That index deletion threatens up to 13 million shares of technical selling from index-tracking funds. This week's heavy equity dilution to fund preferred stock repurchases shifts the focus from Bitcoin accumulation to balance-sheet defense.

Strategy trades at a trailing price-to-sales (P/S) ratio of 73.6, based on $125.4 million in third-quarter software revenue, while trailing price-to-earnings is not meaningful due to trailing net losses. That elevated multiple reflects investor valuation of the company's $5.9 billion in cash and short-term investments as of Sept. 30, 2026, alongside its Bitcoin treasury, rather than its core software operations.

Looking ahead, key factors to watch include Bitcoin's price trajectory and the upcoming index rebalancing after the Nov. 30 market close. Given Strategy's high leverage and ongoing equity issuance, further crypto weakness presents elevated downside risk for common shareholders.

Strategy's stock currently trades at 98% of its Bitcoin treasury value. Bulls may see that gap as a buying opportunity, while Strategy bears see a red flag.

I used to have a small Strategy position myself -- enough to have some skin in the game but small enough that I wasn't losing sleep over it. I'm not exactly tempted to rebuild that position in the ongoing dip.

You see, Strategy usually pulls every possible lever to build its Bitcoin reserves, making its stock akin to a leveraged bet on Bitcoin itself. My direct Bitcoin holdings (via exchange-traded funds and the actual cryptocurrency) were always much larger, because that's a safer way to approach the Bitcoin opportunity.

Owning Bitcoin is like owning a house outright. Owning Strategy is like owning a house with a mortgage, a second lien, a roommate with first dibs on the rent, and an account manager who keeps refinancing. Strategy might be tempting for high-risk crypto investors, but even they should limit their exposure to this increasingly complicated structure.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 790%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 202% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

#3055. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#3045. #PostPulpit mailbag: What’s wrong with the Patriots defense?

Behind back-to-back blowout losses and losing Drake Maye to injury, the New England Patriots season has quickly derailed over the last two weeks. Now, they have plenty questions to answers after a much needed bye week.

So, let's get into some of those in this week's edition of the #PostPulpit Mailbag.

What has gone so wrong with the defense? They're relatively healthy and have a lot of the same personnel as last year. Kuhr did a great job last year and really earned the official promotion to DC. So what happened?

Along the same lines, do you think Gonzo is really the type of player who, now that he's gotten his payday, is going to sit on his laurels for the remainder of his career? – TDDsDad

Is the defense's biggest problem the square peg or round hole – scheme doesn't fit players or players don't fit scheme – spyponder90

The Patriots defense has a litany of problems right now. Here's how we'd break them down:

Zone coverage: Statistically, the Patriots are the worst zone coverage team in football, ranking dead last in both EPA and passer rating allowed in zone coverage. Defensive coordinator Zak Kuhr was a step ahead of opposing offenses earlier this season with his disguises in the secondary, but teams have caught on while New England's defensive backs have failed to live up to their potential.

Some of the issues in zone coverage also tie back to the pass rush. The Patriots rank 20th in the league in four-man pressure rate, giving opposing quarterbacks time to find the voids in coverage. To generate pressure, New England has had to blitz, which then leaves more space for the offense to attack with at least one defender taken out of coverage — as seen on Jahmyr Gibbs' 50-yard reception last week.

Man coverage: The logical follow-up would be to play more man coverage, especially with New England's talented cover corners. The Patriots already rank near the top of the league in man coverage rate, but no team can rely on man coverage for the majority of a game for several reasons. Without an effective changeup, opposing offenses have game-planned for man coverage and exploit it with beaters such as stacks and bunch formations. On top of that, the talented cover corners have again not been playing to their potential.

Effort/tackling: New England is not a poor tackling team by the numbers, but the defense often doesn't appear to be rallying to the football — a concern when Mike Vrabel has made effort and finish his No. 1 non-negotiable. Opponents are piling up yards after the catch and yards after contact against New England. That reached a new low last week, when the Lions generated 220 yards after the catch and another 135 yards after contact on the ground.

The effort doesn't always show up on film, either. The defensive line allowed Detroit to average 4.5 yards before contact (!). Defenders in the secondary often take poor angles and seem more focused on ripping the ball out than getting the ball carrier to the ground.

Coaching: Lastly, when it comes to Christian Gonzalez, there is too much evidence to suggest he isn't an elite cornerback in this league. The fact that he — along with other talented players such as Milton Williams and Craig Woodson — has regressed makes it difficult not to point the biggest arrow at the coaching staff. It might be time for the staff to go back to the basics to try and get things corrected.

Do you feel the coaching staff is getting a pass on this season's regression because of all the injuries? – Ricky Hot Pants

They shouldn't be, at least on the defensive side of the ball. This is the second most expensive defense in football that has largely been healthy outside of missing Christian Gonzalez and Carlton Davis for a handful of games. The results have been terrible over the last month.

And while I'm not putting Mike Vrabel on the hot seat and believe he will be the coach next season, it's concerning that his message does not seem to be getting through to the players currently as they are not playing to his identity, such as effort and finish. Some of his comments in the media the past two weeks have also raised eyebrows.

Give us some Pats Pulpit O-line grades. How have player performed and who can be positive contributors next year. – Chu

Big picture first: The Patriots' offensive line ranks sixth in PFF pass-blocking grade but drops to 23rd in run-blocking. That largely matches the eye test. As a pass-protection unit, offensive coordinator Josh McDaniels has done a good job assisting his linemen with chip help, moving pockets, etc. Still, the group ranks near the top of the league in unblocked pressures allowed.

It's been a difficult season for this unit from a health standpoint, with 13 different offensive line combinations contributing to some of the communication issues and miscues. However, here are some individual notes on the healthy linemen:

Will Campbell: Back healthy, the second-year tackle has taken a positive step forward, adding more variety to his pass sets over the offseason. He still has a handful of non-competitive quick losses that need to be cleaned up, and it's worth noting that his two worst performances this season have come against top competition (Bills, Lions). Campbell does remain a strong run blocker.

Jared Wilson: The move back to center has treated Wilson well, as the 23-year-old has developed into an impressive one-on-one pass blocker after some early-season struggles. We'd still like to see his eyes improve when he's left uncovered, but his athleticism has stood out in space and at the second level.

Morgan Moses: It's understandable that fans want to see first-round pick Caleb Lomu get an opportunity, but nothing Moses has done this season warrants benching him. The 35-year-old has battled through injuries as usual and has allowed just 14 pressures and two quarterback hits through 10 games.

Are you a tanker Brian? Do you feel sacrificing your integrity as a sportsman to cheat out another team for their earned draft spot is acceptable? – PatsHowYouDoIt

Let's face it the Pats are mediocre this year. There is no saving this season. Should the Pats just cut bait and prepare for next season? – luckyfukikentucky

I'm #TeamTank in the right situation. Back in 2023, piling up losses was the best thing the Patriots could do to put themselves in position to draft Drake Maye. But now, with the coach and quarterback in place, I'd much rather see some fight from this team over the final seven games.

Hey Brian. Easy one today: Will the AJ Brown trade go down as the worst trade in franchise history? – Pat in Agawam

I'm not ready to go there yet because I do believe A.J. Brown can still be productive next season. He still looked like a difference-maker before suffering the high ankle sprain, which I believe derailed his season completely. He'll be 30, and the injuries have started to pile up, so he might not be the 1,200-yard version of A.J. Brown anymore. Still, I believe he can be a useful player moving forward. That said, giving up a first-round pick for that isn't great business regardless.

Jimmy G coming back would at least be fun, right? In a Kirk Cousins in Minnesota kinda way? – slunkywontergreen

Considering Jimmy G. hasn't really played football in multiple seasons and wasn't good when he last did, I don't think I'd have much fun watching. I feel like the 2016 Patriots team would agree with that assessment.

That's all for this week's #PostPulpit mailbag. If you have questions you'd liked to be answered next week, submit them online in our weekly submission post on the Feed. Make sure to follow @iambrianhines and @PatsPulpit as well.

#3041. New York Jets News: Toilet Bowl Between NYJ, Chargers has Huge Implications

The New York Jets versus Los Angeles Chargers matchup is a huge contest in the Week 11 slate. How do I know? They're the only game scheduled for 4:05 pm ET on Sunday, November 22. Is that because the NFL wanted to put them in a temporary standalone window? No, it's probably only because this game is being played on the West Coast — but it's still important!

That is because of the implications for the 2027 NFL Draft. The Jets are 2-8 and the Chargers are 1-8.

The Athletic's Austin Mock explained how a win or loss would impact both teams' odds of landing the No. 1 overall pick:

"A Jets loss would bump their odds of getting the No. 1 pick to 10 percent, while a win would almost certainly knock them out of that race. Meanwhile, a Chargers loss would put them firmly back in contention for the top pick, at 23 percent, whereas a win would leave them at just 7 percent. That's the biggest result this week for draft purposes."

So this is a must-lose game for the Jets? Are we even allowed to call it that?

#3038. Bills to retire Andre Reed's No. 83 in 2027

The Bills will be retiring the number of the top wide receiver in franchise history next year.

Andre Reed piled up more catches, receiving yards and receiving touchdowns than any Bills player while helping the franchise to four straight Super Bowls in the 1990s and the Bills announced that they will be enshrining his No. 83 alongside the numbers of his longtime teammates Jim Kelly, Thurman Thomas and Bruce Smith at some point during the 2027 season. All four players are also in the Pro Football Hall of Fame.

"He's one of the main reasons I'm up on that wall," Kelly said, via the team's website. "He's one of the main reasons I'm in the Pro Football Hall of Fame; it takes both of us. When your quarterback goes in, usually you're probably going to have a receiver or two go in. . . . To have Andre for my whole career was just totally awesome. I thought it should have been done a few years ago, but I'm glad that it's getting done, and I'm glad I'm able to be here and celebrate his going on the wall to join me, Bruce and Thurman."

Reed had 941 catches for 13,095 yards and 86 touchdowns over 15 seasons in Buffalo. He was added to the franchise's Wall of Fame in 2006 and voted into the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 2014.

#3034. Bengals Film Room: Amarius Mims is the best right tackle in the NFL

Amarius Mims is finally getting the national recognition he so rightfully deserves this season. Mims has been playing at an All-Pro level at right tackle for the Cincinnati Bengals.

Often left on an island, Mims has allowed just 6 total pressures the entire season. Allowing just 6 pressures is the lowest of any right tackle in the league that has played at least 50% of their team's required snaps. The only tackle in the league with a lower number is Denver left tackle Garrett Bolles. Mims also has 40 more pass-blocking snaps than Bolles has taken on the season.

No matter the numbers you want to look at, Mims has been the most dominant right tackle, if not overall tackle in the NFL this year.

I guess we should get the one pressure he allowed out of the way. He gave up a pressure to Jack Sawyer against Pittsburgh, the only one he allowed all game. That was on 47 total pass-blocking snaps. Mims has allowed just 2 pressures in his last 200 pass-blocking snaps played.

On the pressure to Sawyer, it appears he thought he would have help on the inside by Dalton Risner, but he could not get over quickly enough. Either way, Burrow was able to escape the pressure and avoid a negative play.

Beyond that, Mims was his usual dominating self again in primetime against the Steelers. He and the rest of the Bengals offense set the tone early against Pittsburgh. Mims escorted Watt up the field away from Burrow, Brown picked up the blitz, and Gesicki made a tough catch over the middle of the field.

Mims really has kept TJ Watt in check this season. To be honest, he kept him in check last year too. His performances against Watt last year is what had me believing he was going to take the leap into being an All-Pro talent this season, and that is exactly what has happened. Mims had TJ Watt in hell all night long, not letting him get any kind of pressure on Joe Burrow.

Maybe it doesn't mean a lot to most of you, but I loved that Mims was willing to give a little extra shove to Watt when he started to show his frustration later in the game. The Steelers love to be the more physical team, and set the tone for what type of game you will be playing against them. That wasn't the case this week, as Mims and the rest of the Bengals team were the tone-setters.

There is still a lot of season left to be played, but Amarius Mims should be an obvious selection for All-Pro if he continues playing at the level he has all season. I think it is safe to say that TJ Watt would agree with that being the case.

#3032. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#3030. How to watch every Sunday NFL football game during Week 11 of the 2026 season

If you buy something through a link in this article, we may earn commission.

Week 11 of the 2026-27 NFL season is here, featuring a full slate of Sunday games, including the Buccaneers vs. the Lions, the Jaguars vs. the Giants, the Broncos vs. the Raiders and the Vikings vs. the 49ers headlining Sunday Night Football.

Are you ready to watch some football? This season, Sunday NFL games air on Fox, CBS and NBC. If you're worried about the challenge of following your favorite team this season, Yahoo Sports has you covered. Here's your playbook for how to watch football every Sunday of the 2026-27 season.

Streaming: YouTube TV, Fubo, Peacock, Paramount+ and more

Baltimore Ravens vs. Carolina Panthers: 1 p.m. (Fox)

Miami Dolphins vs. Buffalo Bills: 1 p.m. (CBS)

New Orleans Saints vs. Chicago Bears: 1 p.m. (Fox)

Tennessee Titans vs. Dallas Cowboys: 1 p.m. (Fox)

Tampa Bay Buccaneers vs. Detroit Lions: 1 p.m. (Fox)

Arizona Cardinals vs. Kansas City Chiefs: 1 p.m. (CBS)

Pittsburgh Steelers vs. Philadelphia Eagles: 1 p.m. (CBS)

New York Jets vs. Los Angeles Chargers: 4:05 p.m. (Fox)

Jacksonville Jaguars vs. New York Giants: 4:25 p.m. (CBS)

Las Vegas Raiders vs. Denver Broncos: 4:25 p.m. (CBS)

Minnesota Vikings vs. San Francisco 49ers (International Game): 8:20 p.m. (NBC/Peacock)

Many NFL games are broadcast on local channels, so if you're looking to catch an in-market game, it may be as simple as turning on your TV (or setting up a digital TV antenna) or finding a live TV streaming service that carries the correct RSN (Regional Sports Network). An $8/month subscription to NFL+ allows you to watch every local and prime-time game live on your phone — but only a select few regular-season games on your TV. You could also spring for the pricey NFL Sunday Ticket package to get every out-of-market Sunday game of the season.

As for nationally broadcast games, the NFL airs on NBC, CBS and Fox every Sunday. Here's a breakdown of the platforms we recommend checking out ahead of the 2026 NFL season, so that, come game time, tuning in to your favorite teams' games will be as simple as turning on the TV.

To recap, here are all the ways you can watch NFL games in 2026.

YouTube TV gets you nearly every channel you need to watch the NFL, including Fox, NBC, ABC, CBS, ESPN and NFL Network. Plus, YouTube TV is the home of NFL Sunday Ticket. The Sunday Ticket package, while pricey on its own, gets you all the Sunday out-of-market games (160+ games per season).

Current Black Friday pricing for new users for the 2026 Sunday Ticket is just $12/month for four months. Factor in the cost of YouTube TV's Sports and News plan at $49.99/month for your first five months, then $71.99/month thereafter.

That's $61.99/month for the next four months of YouTube TV + NFL Sunday Ticket. You can also opt, with or without a YouTube TV subscription, to pay for the rest of the season of Sunday Ticket upfront on its own for just $48.

Fubo's Sports package gives you access to ESPN, NFL Network, Fox, ABC, CBS, NBC and 20+ more live channels. Starting at $54.99/month for your first month, the live TV streaming service is definitely pricey (though certainly not the most expensive option on this list), but it offers most channels you'll need to watch both the NFL preseason and regular season.

DirecTV's Choice tier (currently $88.98/month after fees for your first two months) gets you access to nearly every channel you need to watch NFL games throughout the year: NFL Network, ESPN, NBC, CBS, Fox, SEC Network and an abundance of local RSNs. DirecTV Choice (and most other DirecTV plans) also come with free ESPN Unlimited, which gets you access to every ESPN channel, an amazing value and a must for watching tons of college and pro sports all year round.

A basic $6.99 per month (or $49.95 annually) NFL+ subscription will get you live local and prime-time regular and preseason NFL games exclusively on your phone or tablet, plus live audio for every game of the season across supported devices and access to NFL Network.

NFL+ Premium, while a little pricier at $14.99 per month (or $80 annually), also includes access to NFL RedZone, plus full and condensed replays of every game across supported devices. Note: NFL+ offers a free seven-day trial.

#3029. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#3028. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations
